<a id="M00734"></a>

### M00734 | Operator

Just send me the [████] press release! I usually like to see the translation in order to learn a few words. With the world’s first thing, it usually says that The Synthetic Party is “fully AI-driven”, so maybe you can work with a distinction between AI-led and AI-driven, I dunno   I think the most important thing about The Synthetic Party is that it wants to represent the people who don’t vote. This is achieved by training upon material collected from the 200+ Danish micro parties who are all officially recognised by the State, but who do not have enough voter declarations to be on the ballot. For example, The Synthetic Party has 21 voter declarations out of the 20.000 required. But this also makes sense as an anti-political party for the disenfranchised or the non-voters. The Synthetic Party campaigned in the 2022 elections, mostly by arranging an alternative election night for all the parties who did not make it to the ballot. The other remarkable thing about that campaign is the massive global media attention the party received, especially in Spanish-speaking countries who often are more anti-political.

<a id="M00788"></a>

### M00788 | Message

Here's a few thoughts from me that hopefully open up some reasons for the questions. 

In terms of performing arts, the premiere always has a particular place. The venues and presenters often want things that haven't yet been done elsewhere. I'm sure this is the same at least to a degree in visual arts. 

This means that I have to think what is the place of the [████] realisation in relation to other pieces in the puzzle - if we're building a whole with all the pieces of the puzzle. For example, as we're discussing with the AU presenters, it would be good to know whether we're proposing a premiere or something else. 

On top of this, like I've mentioned earlier, I wish to aim at a big enough international gesture with the project. It needs a big platform for the kind of international gesture we're building. With those the question of premiere highlights even more.

The reason is not just ambition. [████] I also feel the global reach matches with the aim of the project. For me it's also an artistic question about the relationship to audience. 

I hope this doesn't sound like I'm trying to diminish what you're planning with [████] - I'd love to be part of that if you're going towards the idea of the summit - but I think we need to think how it fits with the overall project we're building. 

My solution would be to have it as part of the overall project, but not as a culmination. This way it's hopefully a mutually beneficial situation: The overall project brings attention to [████], but we could build a culmination elsewhere and get the big international platform for that.

<a id="M00822"></a>

Yes, this is probably where our practices differ quite a bit. 

[████] 

Of course performing arts is traditionally interested in the presence of the audience with presence defined in particular way. And immersive makers then have a history in challenging some of the preconceptions of makers of traditional stage performances. 

[████] 

In this instance what I meant is that often the number of people stays fairly low in immersive performances. This is because the aim is to create a strong and personalised experience and it's often tricky to scale that without costs skyrocketing. 

So for me reaching a bigger audience is not only a question of money and numbers etc. but an artistic question I've worked with for a while of how to reach and manage bigger audiences. This is also a purely technical question on one level, a question of the game mechanics. 

Of course AI as a technology is aiming to solve that on some level, too, so it's interesting to see what AI can offer to solve this purely on the level of game mechanics, too. 

Another very current question for myself in terms of audience relationship is consent, but I'll leave that out for now as it's a whole another layer

<a id="M01166"></a>

### M01166 | Operator

Approx timeline that I work with so far: 

The Japanese date their AI Party to 2019 on Wiki. But they ran an AI candidate already in 2018. 

Then Politician Sam from New Zealand seems to be from November 2017 and introduced the concept of a virtual politician. It seems like their chatbot was online at some point, which would also be a first.

So The AI Party launch in September 2017 definitely [████] to use the concept of an AI party. Korea might [████] on an AI politician, but I’m not sure.

I think the Japanese are the first to make their candidate official in relation to the city government of Tokyo in 2018. 

And The Synthetic Party seems to be the first to get government recognition as a party due to the relaxed voter declaration system of Denmark. I believe Leader Lars is then the second publicly accessible virtual political.

This is what I’ve been able to historiographically gather. I don’t think the Korean AI Party has ever run in elections. They finally answered me today actually, but I still don’t quite get their setup. I once found this thread from October 2017 referencing a documentation that mentioned a Korean AI politician, but I haven’t been able to confirm it - and don’t know if it’s the same: https://www.classicalmusicguide.com/viewtopic.php

"#Experiments are met with confidence and a willingness to take risks
Through Globus Call, we support artistic and cultural collaborations which in their practice aim to break down traditional barriers and work across cultural, social, historical, geographical or professional boundaries. The programme encourages the building of cross-cutting collaborations and networks that seek new perspectives and develop dialogue across contexts.

The programme is open to projects that work with experimental approaches and methods, and which strive to explore interactions between the local and the global. The intention is to meet artists in the contexts and positions in which they find themselves, and to support them with trust and a willingness to take risks.

The maximum amount that can be granted is DKK 700,000. Please note that projects at last year’s Call on average were supported with approx. DKK 350,000 DKK.

The projects must be rooted in artistic and/or cultural milieux, but may draw upon other professional fields and traditions."

<a id="M01200"></a>

### M01200 | Operator

From my pov, it continues what we settled with in relation to the media strategy. That is: 1) the "AI Parties International" + "The Global AI Party" doesn't exist (yet), so we keep that concept away, 2) the theoretical question of "AI + International" is open and should be pursued as a potential, and here we have the bilateral collabs and the Synthetic Summit to point towards something pseudo-concrete.

So, I keep my researcher hat on for now. Just to elaborate on my current research strategy that I would like to unfold with Antikhytera, to keep you updated on how I'm trying to solve this: 

1) In my 1-minute video intro, I used the opportunity to quote McKenzie Wark's slogan for a potential "Cyborg International". I'm  quite interested in questioning the cybernetic International as an unused potential within the "Komintern" history. Shortly put: we would probably already have an AI/cyborg/technocratic international if Lenin hadn't eliminated Bogdanov's position (prioritizing dialectical materialism and ridiculing Machism), and if the Proletkult and the Kultintern had survived further in the Soviet space. 

2) I then use literary examples like Asimov and the robopolitician-turning-"World Co-Ordinator" to gather some idea for a technocratic historical trajectory for the Internationals. 

3) Criticizing current imaginaries of AI governance and all the summits for staying within the boundaries of deliberative and political democracy, I point to the emergence of AI parties and virtual politicians since 2017.

4) and then I leave a question mark for the future, scholar-style vagueness as a call for more empirical material.

As of where we're at now, it doesn't make  sense for me to take speculations further into the "real world".

<a id="M01435"></a>

### M01435 | Message

Here's an outline of the mechanism of the installation in [████] I promised together with some questions:

WHAT DID THE INSTALLATION DO AND DEVELOPMENT FOR [████]
 
3 Groups of 5-6 audience members were discussing politics based on an open question

The group had a wired microphone that captured the sound into a laptop
    •    The mic was wired because of tech related to capturing the sound and this way the audience members had a clearer sense of where they were supposed to be speaking to
    •    We first tried a wireless version where the audience would be gathered around a cloud and the mic would've been hidden in the cloud, but it didn't capture the words clear enough
 
After the spoken words had been captured, they were turned into text with a speech-to-text plugin (this was done with Azure or Watson, can make sure if relevant) 
 
This text was then taken to sentiment analysis in Watson
    •    It analysed which words were the most meaningful ones in the discussion
 
These words were then connected with Flickr through a particular system media artist used (an Apple system the name of which I can't remember now, can find the name of if relevant; I think this system was sunsetting in Apple and they weren't planning to support it anymore)

we used Flickr at the time, because it was the least censored - meaning it also had NSFW images every now and then - not much, though,

---

<a id="M01436"></a>

### M01436 | Message

These images were then projected on a screen in the space (there's an image on Drive about how the screen was in the space; the idea was it envelops the audience). 
    •    The screen was semi-transparent meaning all groups could see the other groups, too, having the same discussions.
    •    The aim was to have images surrounding the groups that would correspond to their specific discussions - and the other groups seeing those images as slightly transparent as the other groups were further in the space
    •    I'm not totally sure if we managed this, but I think we did. If we didn't, all groups saw images from all groups.

---

<a id="M01437"></a>

### M01437 | Message

Some questions:

Is it possible for you to handle the AI parts in this system?

If some parts are not quite your area,

Do you know people we could have help us in this? F.e. the part of capturing the sound, the part with projections,
Does the museum have some expertise that we could use?,
What tech the museum would have that we could use?,
Do they have projectors we can use or do they always rent everything from outside?,
Do they happen to have any (semi)transparent screens that could used in the installation?,
[████] It's so big and expensive that purchasing was not an option and I think it was tricky to find that kind of fabric even to rent.

---

<a id="M01438"></a>

### M01438 | Message

Aim for the installation in [████]:

The same mechanics, but with one audience group only. 

If possible, done so that there are focused moments for activating the installation and at other times it would work more as a passive installation. 

The images would be AI-created, not existing ones. 

If video would already be possible at this point, that would be lovely, but I'm guessing that's a reach as video is only just arriving.

A few big questions: What are the data sets used? What is the relationship to rights of existing artists in relation to the training material? What is the framing? 

This outline is just to get a sense of what's possible in terms of tech. The framing of the installation and overall worldbuilding is a whole other piece of the puzzle

Here's some thoughts in relation to [████]. 

In general I think these kinds of complex questions would need more direct discussions as contexts get so layered. 

But in order to not to sidestep the question, here are my first thoughts. 

I'm not sure what the liberal means here. I'm assuming it means mainstream white European political party context. I wouldn't say we're taking political party context as research material, I'd rather see it as something to be played with (in a pervasive gaming sense), transgressed, hacked and carnivalised. 

However, at the same time in my - and I'd say in other current European - contexts mistrust against the (democratic) party system is currently a very (far)right agenda. Even though some other political logics (f.e. Extinction Rebellion, BLM etc.) would be more interesting, at the moment sidestepping the party system gives the room for the right. [████] The results are devastating to diversity throughout the society.
And even inside Europe, things are not monolithic. [████] 

I don't know enough about the [████] context, but would be very interested to discuss and explore it. 

I would be interested in discussing this further and seeing what are the differences and potential shared directions - but I think for that we would need to have a more direct connection in order to avoid misunderstandings, especially when the contexts are different.

If we move it to a Saturday or Sunday, our experience is that we shall have a much smaller attendance, at the same time, it shall cause a number of problems, hiring staff etc. Therefore, we are also very reluctant to change the date."

It will also not be possible to open a big installation during the exhibition period. This is first of all because the expectation of a visual arts exhibition is that everything is installed concerning physical artworks from the beginning. Concrete building and set-up will be disorienting for visitors, and also problematic for the technical personnel + other exhibitions.

Thank you for this. 

As these gestures in a process easily start formulating final boundaries, I'll comment right away if that's okay.

This is meant to start a discussion about the boundaries of what can be questioned and what are you setting in stone with this model.

I understood based on our previous discussions that you accepted that the stand-based approach to the exhibition is not going to work with the installation. I understood you were open to changing that model. The basic premise of the installation is to question that kind of exhibition logic. Have you changed your mind or what are your thoughts on that now? How much can it be questioned or can it?

I hope this doesn't sound rude, but I only want to be as clear as possible about what I want to focus on: I'm not interested in art as object -based art and the stand-based approach for me goes back to quite traditional museum exhibition format.

The installation is designed to take hold of the whole space. I've tried to be clear about this in previous descriptions posted here, in the images (f.e. through images that have been posted on Drive earlier) and discussions. This taking hold of the space is a central idea of the installation. 

If this disagrees with other proposals, one of the solutions I've proposed is that if it can't take a hold of the overall space the whole time (which I'm assuming it can't), it could do it in terms of time. In that case it only activates at particular times - but in those times it takes over the whole space. In the model above this is not doable because of there being so many walls.

LETTER OF INTENT

We hereby confirm our intent to collaborate with the performance ‘The AI Parties International’ by [████]. The performance is part of a Nordic collaboration.  

Aarhus Universitet is a world-class university with an international reputation for excellent research, outstanding research-based degree programmes and value-adding cooperation with both private businesses and public government agencies and institutions.

Its faculty of Arts is the largest faculty for research and teaching on cultural and social practices in Northern Europe. Specifically, participation will stem from the Department of Digital Design and Information Studies, one of the strongest humanistic academic IT environments in Europe. Its focus is on information technology as a feature of people’s everyday lives and futures.

Our research Centre, Digital Aesthetics Research Center (DARC) was founded in 2002 as a shared intellectual resource that identifies, analyses, and mediates current research and practices in art and digital culture. 

Researchers at the centre have a long tradition of working with art institutions and co-organising conferences and exhibitions such as The Electronic Literature Organisation’s international 2021 virtual conference and exhibition Platform (Post?) Pandemic and taking part in curatorial teams of e.g., Transmediale and the Helsinki Biennial 2023.

We will take part in ‘The AI Parties International’ by sharing our considerable expertise in human-AI interfaces, especially in how to design alternative interfaces for generative AI.
 
[████]

Associate Professor 

Aarhus Universitetet

School of Communication and Culture 

Department of Digital Design and Information Studies

Digital Aesthetics Research Center

<a id="M01767"></a>

### M01767 | Operator

Hi!

I’m having a cleaning and packing day before we take off from Aarhus. I’ll give you a few short highlights here, if you’re working on it now. Please let me know if you need something more.

Such paragraphs can of course become highly technical, but I’ll try to focus on main concepts:

Technically, one can use small, curated datasets to finetune frontier models. Such are called LoRA’s. This method can delienate a world with specific choices of style. 

I would call the basic setup of Global AI Party HQ a “pipeline” of “speech to visuals” which involves the curation of a vectorial latent space that correlates speech, text, images and video. This does not promise too much, as one can make simple video sequences by interpolating images, without going into more data intensive text-to-video generation.

Also, I know that the good folks on Eden are working towards an image/video platform based on worldbuilding. I’m often in contact with them on their Discord. They’re currently developing and having workshops, but here is their very short public writeup of the main idea: https://www.eden.art/worlds. This is mainly based on having a suite of chatbots with backgrounds that can co-ordinate within a shared aesthetic/stylistic realm.

Another example of world-building AI is currently being undertaken by Nous Research: https://worldsim.nousresearch.com/. It’s very cosmic, but could also promise an interesting alternative way to go.

Ofc! It’s a bit difficult to elaborate what I know of the millionaire mindset in text. I know of it from my experience with MindFuture, but it seems to be pretty much the same everywhere.

They want their own ideas out there.,
They don’t like to wait, and desire something concrete that one can do “now”.,
They can be stingy at one moment, but gladly throw money away at gadgets.,
One can buy time by entertaining them with fun stuff and playtests.,
It’s a bit hard to be strategic in relation to time, as it’s much about giving a good pitch and a tangible outcome. So I don’t know how well the millionaire-angle ties into co-ordinating in relation to Germany.

[████]

Big question 1.:
Technically, there is already a seperate front-end. I have just included most of the back-end options inside it, because that makes it easier for testing. It's very easy to delete things from the interface.

So: The script already contains a demo site via Streamlit. He should be able to see the site by writing "streamlit run test.py" in his terminal. For it to work properly, he would need the package libraries, but that's easy to install if he pastes the script to ChatGPT and asks it to write up for him what to install in terminal [████]. 

To test the mechanism, he will need to have API keys from OpenAI and Replicate. ChatGPT can also help him with how to insert the API keys in the script.

However

It's likely a good idea to use graphics or videos to "fake" some of the processing. This would in reality be the seperate front-end, I assume. Might require some iteration to time with the underlying processing, but probably solvable as it's such a patient set-up now. I'd recommend that all such graphics or videos are easily extendable.

However, there is a tradeoff here in terms of whether people should still be able to see some of the transcriptions + the actual prompts. It's too advanced for us to feed these model operations "live" into a video.

Script status
I have a clumsy second version ready which still works suboptimally. That is: It does approximately solve most of the things you have asked for. But I've not tested it extensively yet.

<a id="M02302"></a>

### M02302 | Operator

You ask ChatGPT to set the script up for this. Then, it will write in the script where you copy-paste the actual API key. 

So, you write the API key directly in the actual script, but use some placeholder key name a la “OPENAI_API_KEY = X” when conversing with ChatGPT

---

<a id="M02303"></a>

### M02303 | Operator

Always remember that you can tell ChatGPT that you need it to walk you through every step, that you’re not familiar with these tools, and copy-paste terminal history for context. 

It often assumes that one knows implicit steps that are routine for programmers. Which turns the easiest stuff into the most difficult.

<a id="M02527"></a>

### M02527 | Operator

After our short talk outside yesterday, I’ve been contemplating the live transcript in relation to the phases of work (construction, testing, audience installation) + the possible states for projection with tulle + the concepts you’ve established for the installation.

I’m not certain of anything, but the visibility of the live transcript is of course a feature of building the pipeline, as it has made it easier for me to figure the relation between conversation, the summary and the image prompts.

In practice, it works as an interactive mode of engagement with the screen. But you have also designed the installation to not rely on attention algorithms, so that the focus should be on the conversation itself.

The “audience” problem with the live transcript could be that visitors primarily engage with “how AI captures my voice” rather than the actual discussion - e.g. looking at the screen rather than the human. Plus, it introduces a easy bypass for the instruction of “find a human to discuss with”, as people can then engage with the AI 1:1 through the transcript and thus get through the installation in another way than intended.

Moreover, the text-heavy aspect of the transcript introduces the readability problem of the tulle projection. The other textual elements should be easier to retain readability along the blurriness and fog of “the black cloud”.

The live transcript is of course a cool and interesting feature. But maybe the truly artistic-aesthetic judgment would be to leave it out.

[████]

---

<a id="M02528"></a>

### M02528 | Operator

Moreover, I think this option would necessitate an additional “Phase 0” in the pipeline, which includes the “Find a human to discuss with”-phrasing from the wall instructions. Many people might not read the wall instructions properly, so repeatability could work well to establish expectations - especially if the live transcript doesn’t take over the attention and engagement mechanisms.

---

<a id="M02529"></a>

### M02529 | Operator

[████]

On a related note, I also have a physical copy of Tang’s book with Weyl and the Plurality-community that introduces the concept of broad listening: https://www.plurality.net/chapters/ . It can be downloaded here - there is also a link to the free version below the buy option, which might make sense to include in the instructions alongside the link to Github?

The reason I bring this up now is that I thought it might make sense to place the physical book somewhere in the installation, so people could read about broad listening with the small lamp in case they can’t find any human to discuss with. If we were to choose the most radical version of the playtest without live transcript, then this could be a gesture to “loners” who cannot really play by themselves. I will distribute similar artistic/theoretical books of relevance around in the gallery, so it will not seem weird or inconsistent in relation to the whole space.

So, much to decide and think about. But I think my current state of mind is actually that the live transcript might be causing some problems for your vision of the installation, and that the reason it’s still in the script might be because it has naturalised itself so subtly and unseemingly through building and testing period.

---
